```
1→"""Predicates and small utilities for work queue items."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import re
     6→from fnmatch import fnmatch
     7→
     8→from desloppify.core.enums import finding_status_tokens
     9→from desloppify.core.registry import DETECTORS
    10→from desloppify.intelligence.integrity import (
    11→    is_holistic_subjective_finding,
    12→)
    13→
    14→ALL_STATUSES = set(finding_status_tokens(include_all=True))
    15→ACTION_TYPE_PRIORITY = {"auto_fix": 0, "refactor": 1, "manual_fix": 2, "reorganize": 3}
    16→ATTEST_EXAMPLE = (
    17→    "I have actually [DESCRIBE THE CONCRETE CHANGE YOU MADE] "
    18→    "and I am not gaming the score by resolving without fixing."
    19→)
    20→
    21→
    22→def detail_dict(item: dict) -> dict:
    23→    """Return finding detail as a dict; tolerate legacy/non-dict payloads."""
    24→    detail = item.get("detail")
    25→    return detail if isinstance(detail, dict) else {}
    26→
    27→
    28→def status_matches(item_status: str, status_filter: str) -> bool:
    29→    return status_filter == "all" or item_status == status_filter
    30→
    31→
    32→def is_subjective_finding(item: dict) -> bool:
    33→    detector = item.get("detector")
    34→    if detector in {"subjective_assessment"}:
    35→        return True
    36→    if detector == "holistic_review":
    37→        return True
    38→    return False
    39→
    40→
    41→def is_review_finding(item: dict) -> bool:
    42→    return item.get("detector") == "review"
    43→
    44→
    45→def is_subjective_queue_item(item: dict) -> bool:
    46→    """True for subjective work items, including collapsed subjective clusters."""
    47→    if item.get("kind") == "subjective_dimension":
    48→        return True
    49→    if item.get("kind") == "cluster":
    50→        members = item.get("members", [])
    51→        return bool(members) and all(
    52→            m.get("kind") == "subjective_dimension" for m in members
    53→        )
    54→    return False
    55→
    56→
    57→def review_finding_weight(item: dict) -> float:
    58→    """Return review issue weight aligned with issues list ordering."""
    59→    confidence = str(item.get("confidence", "low")).lower()
    60→    weight_by_confidence = {
    61→        "high": 1.0,
    62→        "medium": 0.7,
    63→        "low": 0.3,
    64→    }
    65→    weight = weight_by_confidence.get(confidence, 0.3)
    66→    if detail_dict(item).get("holistic"):
    67→        weight *= 10.0
    68→    return float(weight)
    69→
    70→
    71→def scope_matches(item: dict, scope: str | None) -> bool:
    72→    """Apply show-style pattern matching against a queue item."""
    73→    if not scope:
    74→        return True
    75→
    76→    item_id = item.get("id", "")
    77→    detector = item.get("detector", "")
    78→    filepath = item.get("file", "")
    79→    summary = item.get("summary", "")
    80→    dimension = detail_dict(item).get("dimension_name", "")
    81→    kind = item.get("kind", "")
    82→
    83→    if "*" in scope:
    84→        return any(
    85→            fnmatch(candidate, scope)
    86→            for candidate in (item_id, filepath, detector, dimension, summary)
    87→        )
    88→
    89→    if "::" in scope:
    90→        return item_id.startswith(scope)
    91→
    92→    lowered = scope.lower()
    93→    if kind == "subjective_dimension":
    94→        return (
    95→            lowered in item_id.lower()
    96→            or lowered in dimension.lower()
    97→            or lowered in summary.lower()
    98→        )
    99→
   100→    # Hash suffix: 8+ hex chars matches the tail segment of a finding ID.
   101→    if len(lowered) >= 8 and re.fullmatch(r"[0-9a-f]+", lowered):
   102→        return item_id.lower().endswith("::" + lowered)
   103→
   104→    return (
   105→        detector == scope
   106→        or filepath == scope
   107→        or filepath.startswith(scope.rstrip("/") + "/")
   108→    )
   109→
   110→
   111→def slugify(text: str) -> str:
   112→    return re.sub(r"[^a-z0-9_]+", "_", text.lower()).strip("_")
   113→
   114→
   115→def supported_fixers_for_item(state: dict, item: dict) -> set[str] | None:
   116→    """Return supported fixers for an item's language when known."""
   117→    lang = str(item.get("lang", "") or "").strip()
   118→    if not lang:
   119→        return None
   120→
   121→    caps = state.get("lang_capabilities", {})
   122→    if not isinstance(caps, dict):
   123→        return None
   124→
   125→    lang_caps = caps.get(lang, {})
   126→    if not isinstance(lang_caps, dict):
   127→        return None
   128→
   129→    fixers = lang_caps.get("fixers")
   130→    if not isinstance(fixers, list):
   131→        return None
   132→    return {fixer for fixer in fixers if isinstance(fixer, str)}
   133→
   134→
   135→def primary_command_for_finding(
   136→    item: dict, *, supported_fixers: set[str] | None = None
   137→) -> str:
   138→    detector = item.get("detector", "")
   139→    meta = DETECTORS.get(detector)
   140→    if meta and meta.action_type == "auto_fix" and meta.fixers:
   141→        available_fixers = [
   142→            fixer
   143→            for fixer in meta.fixers
   144→            if supported_fixers is not None and fixer in supported_fixers
   145→        ]
   146→        if available_fixers:
   147→            return f"desloppify autofix {available_fixers[0]} --dry-run"
   148→    if detector == "subjective_review":
   149→        if is_holistic_subjective_finding(item):
   150→            return "desloppify review --prepare"
   151→        return "desloppify show subjective"
   152→    return f'desloppify plan resolve "{item.get("id", "")}" --note "<what you did>" --confirm'
   153→
   154→
   155→__all__ = [
   156→    "ALL_STATUSES",
   157→    "ATTEST_EXAMPLE",
   158→    "detail_dict",
   159→    "is_review_finding",
   160→    "is_subjective_finding",
   161→    "is_subjective_queue_item",
   162→    "primary_command_for_finding",
   163→    "review_finding_weight",
   164→    "scope_matches",
   165→    "slugify",
   166→    "status_matches",
   167→    "supported_fixers_for_item",
   168→]
```
```
   169→
```

```
1→"""Unified queue-resolution context.
     2→
     3→A frozen ``QueueContext`` computed once per operation replaces the scattered
     4→``plan`` / ``target_strict`` / ``policy`` threading through function chains.
     5→Callers build one context and pass it everywhere — makes the wrong thing
     6→impossible.
     7→"""
     8→
     9→from __future__ import annotations
    10→
    11→from dataclasses import dataclass
    12→
    13→from desloppify.engine._plan.subjective_policy import (
    14→    SubjectiveVisibility,
    15→    compute_subjective_visibility,
    16→)
    17→from desloppify.engine._state.schema import StateModel
    18→
    19→# Sentinel: "auto-load plan from disk" (the default).
    20→_PLAN_AUTO_LOAD = object()
    21→
    22→
    23→@dataclass(frozen=True)
    24→class QueueContext:
    25→    """Immutable snapshot of resolved queue parameters.
    26→
    27→    Built once via :func:`queue_context`, then threaded through
    28→    ``build_work_queue``, ``plan_aware_queue_breakdown``, and command
    29→    helpers so every call site agrees on plan, target, and policy.
    30→    """
    31→
    32→    plan: dict | None
    33→    target_strict: float
    34→    policy: SubjectiveVisibility
    35→
    36→
    37→def queue_context(
    38→    state: StateModel,
    39→    *,
    40→    config: dict | None = None,
    41→    plan: dict | None | object = _PLAN_AUTO_LOAD,
    42→    target_strict: float | None = None,
    43→) -> QueueContext:
    44→    """Build a :class:`QueueContext` with all parameters resolved.
    45→
    46→    Resolution order:
    47→
    48→    1. **plan** — explicit value wins; sentinel ``_PLAN_AUTO_LOAD`` triggers
    49→       ``load_plan()`` (guarded by ``PLAN_LOAD_EXCEPTIONS``).
    50→    2. **target_strict** — explicit float wins; ``None`` reads from *config*
    51→       via ``target_strict_score_from_config``; final fallback is ``95.0``.
    52→    3. **policy** — ``compute_subjective_visibility(state, ...)`` with the
    53→       resolved plan and target_strict so every downstream consumer sees
    54→       the same objective-vs-subjective balance.
    55→    """
    56→    # --- resolve plan ---
    57→    if plan is _PLAN_AUTO_LOAD:
    58→        from desloppify.core.exception_sets import PLAN_LOAD_EXCEPTIONS
    59→        from desloppify.engine.plan import load_plan
    60→
    61→        try:
    62→            resolved_plan: dict | None = load_plan()
    63→        except PLAN_LOAD_EXCEPTIONS:
    64→            resolved_plan = None
    65→    else:
    66→        resolved_plan = plan  # type: ignore[assignment]
    67→
    68→    # --- resolve target_strict ---
    69→    if target_strict is not None:
    70→        resolved_target = target_strict
    71→    elif config is not None:
    72→        from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.score import (
    73→            target_strict_score_from_config,
    74→        )
    75→
    76→        resolved_target = target_strict_score_from_config(config, fallback=95.0)
    77→    else:
    78→        resolved_target = 95.0
    79→
    80→    # --- resolve policy ---
    81→    resolved_policy = compute_subjective_visibility(
    82→        state,
    83→        target_strict=resolved_target,
    84→        plan=resolved_plan,
    85→    )
    86→
    87→    return QueueContext(
    88→        plan=resolved_plan,
    89→        target_strict=resolved_target,
    90→        policy=resolved_policy,
    91→    )
    92→
    93→
    94→__all__ = [
    95→    "QueueContext",
    96→    "queue_context",
    97→]
    98→
```
```

     3→Work-queue internals live in ``desloppify.engine._work_queue``; this module
     4→exposes the stable, non-private API used by commands, rendering helpers, and
     5→test suites.
     6→"""
     7→
     8→from __future__ import annotations
     9→
    10→# --- context: unified queue-resolution context -----------------------------
    11→from desloppify.engine._work_queue.context import (
    12→    QueueContext,
    13→    queue_context,
    14→)
    15→
    16→# --- core: queue building & options ----------------------------------------
    17→from desloppify.engine._work_queue.core import (
    18→    ATTEST_EXAMPLE,
    19→    QueueBuildOptions,
    20→    WorkQueueResult,
    21→    build_work_queue,
    22→    group_queue_items,
    23→)
    24→
    25→# --- helpers: status/scope matching, item utilities ------------------------

    28→    is_review_finding,
    29→    is_subjective_finding,

    33→    scope_matches,

    36→    supported_fixers_for_item,
    37→)
    38→
    39→# --- synthetic: dimension items, triage stages, workflow builders ----------
    40→from desloppify.engine._work_queue.synthetic import (
    41→    build_subjective_items,
    42→    subjective_strict_scores,
    43→)
    44→
    45→# --- issues: review-finding work queue -------------------------------------
    46→from desloppify.engine._work_queue.issues import (

    48→    impact_label,
    49→    list_open_review_findings,
    50→    update_investigation,
    51→)
    52→
    53→# --- ranking: sort keys, grouping ------------------------------------------
```

Now I have a thorough understanding of the code. Let me provide my analysis.

---

## Evaluation of the Proposed Plan

I've read every file in the `_work_queue/` package and traced all import edges. Here is my assessment, addressing each of your four proposals, then the additional questions you asked.

---

### 1. Extract pipeline steps from `build_work_queue` -- Assessment: Good with refinements

**Your proposed orchestrator shape is correct.** The current `build_work_queue` is 180 lines (including docstring) with the pipeline shape buried under inline detail. Extracting into named private functions will make the pipeline scannable.

**Edge cases and issues I see in the proposed code:**

**a) `_resolve_plan_and_policy` conflates too much.** Your proposal has this returning `(plan, policy)`, but in the current code, plan resolution also involves `scan_path`, `status`, and `subjective_threshold` validation (lines 120-133). Policy resolution depends on `ctx` *and* `resolved_options.policy` *and* `state` (lines 148-152). I'd split this into two functions:

- `_resolve_context(opts, state) -> tuple[dict | None, str | None, str, float]` -- returns `(plan, scan_path, status, threshold)`. This resolves the context/option precedence and validates.
- Keep policy resolution inline (it's 4 lines) or fold it into `_gather_subjective_items` where it's actually consumed.

Actually, on reflection, policy is also used for the return -- no wait, it isn't. Policy is only used inside `_gather_subjective_items`. So the cleanest split is: `_resolve_plan_and_policy` computes `effective_plan` and `policy`, and pass `scan_path`/`status`/`threshold` as resolved locals. The threshold validation is too small to extract.

**b) `_gather_finding_items` is trivially thin.** It would just be `build_finding_items(state, scan_path=..., status_filter=..., scope=..., chronic=...)`. A one-line delegation to another module isn't a useful abstraction unless you want to hide the parameter threading. I'd keep this call inline in the orchestrator. It's already a single named function call.

**c) `_gather_subjective_items` is the right extraction.** Lines 154-180 are 27 lines of subjective policy gating with plan-override logic. This is the most valuable extraction -- it has real logic (scope filtering, policy check, plan_queue_set bypass). Good name.

**d) `_gather_workflow_items` is the right extraction.** Lines 183-197 are 15 lines of conditional injection. The pattern of 5 builders with `if not None` guards is the kind of monotony that belongs behind a name.

**e) `_passes_impact_floor` -- I'd make this a predicate function rather than using it in a list comprehension.** Current code (lines 202-210) is a 7-line compound boolean. Extracting it as:

```python
_MIN_IMPACT = 0.05

def _passes_impact_floor(item: dict) -> bool:
    if item.get("kind") != "finding":
        return True
    if item.get("is_review") or item.get("is_subjective"):
        return True
    impact = item.get("estimated_impact")
    return not impact or impact >= _MIN_IMPACT
```

This reads much better than the current negated-or chain.

**f) `_apply_plan_pipeline` and `_finalize_plan_order` -- naming concern.** Your proposed names `_apply_plan_pipeline` and `_finalize_plan_order` are both vague. The current code has two clearly delineated plan-aware blocks:

- **Pre-sort** (lines 214-222): compute new_ids, enrich metadata, stamp sort keys, separate skipped
- **Post-sort** (lines 226-232): re-append skipped, stamp positions, filter cluster

I'd name these `_plan_presort(items, state, plan)` and `_plan_postsort(items, skipped, plan, opts)`. The "pre/post" relative to `sort()` makes the contract immediately clear. But these are small -- you could also keep them inline since they're just calling plan_order functions in sequence. The plan_order functions already have good names; wrapping them adds an indirection layer. I'll address this more below.

**g) `_maybe_run_scan_item` -- good extraction.** The "inject run-scan when empty" block (lines 237-248) is a self-contained concern. I'd return a list (empty or one item) so the caller does `items += _maybe_run_scan_item(items, plan)`.

**h) Your proposed orchestrator has a subtle correctness issue.** In the proposed code:

```python
new_ids, skipped = _apply_plan_pipeline(items, state, plan)
items.sort(key=item_sort_key)
_finalize_plan_order(items, skipped, plan, opts)
```

But in the current code, the plan pipeline only runs `if effective_plan:` (lines 216, 226). Your extraction must handle the no-plan case gracefully (return empty new_ids and empty skipped). That's fine, just ensure the extraction has an early return for `plan is None`.

**My recommended orchestrator shape:**

```python
def build_work_queue(state, *, options=None):
    opts = options or QueueBuildOptions()
    ctx = opts.context
    plan = ctx.plan if ctx is not None else opts.plan
    scan_path, status, threshold = _resolve_inputs(opts, state)

    # 1. Gather items from all sources
    items = build_finding_items(state, scan_path=scan_path, status_filter=status,
                                scope=opts.scope, chronic=opts.chronic)
    items += _gather_subjective_items(state, opts, plan, threshold)
    items += _gather_workflow_items(state, plan, status)

    # 2. Score and filter
    enrich_with_impact(items, state.get("dimension_scores", {}))
    items = [i for i in items if _passes_impact_floor(i)]

    # 3. Plan-aware ordering
    new_ids, skipped = _stamp_plan_keys(items, state, plan)
    items.sort(key=item_sort_key)
    _apply_plan_postsort(items, skipped, plan, opts)

    # 4. Finalize
    items += _empty_queue_fallback(plan)
    return _truncate_and_wrap(items, new_ids, opts)
```

Key differences from your proposal:
- `build_finding_items` call stays inline (it's already a named function, wrapping adds nothing).
- `_resolve_inputs` returns the three validated scalars, keeping plan resolution as 2 lines at the top.
- `_stamp_plan_keys` is clearer than `_apply_plan_pipeline` -- it says what happens.
- `_apply_plan_postsort` rather than `_finalize_plan_order` -- "postsort" says when, not just "finalize".
- `_truncate_and_wrap` handles count limit + explain + return dict construction.
- `_empty_queue_fallback` takes only `plan` (it doesn't need `items` -- it's called after items is potentially empty, so check `not items` at the call site).

Actually wait -- `_empty_queue_fallback` needs to know whether items is empty. Let me reconsider. You could either pass `items` or just do the check at the call site:

```python
if not items:
    items += _empty_queue_fallback(plan)
```

That's clean.

### 2. Named sort tier constants -- Assessment: Good, with a structural clarification

Your proposed constants are correct in value but the naming conflates two different ranking dimensions:

**Plan-level grouping (used in `item_sort_key`):**
```python
_TIER_PLANNED = 0   # Has explicit _plan_position
_TIER_EXISTING = 1  # Known item, no plan position
_TIER_NEW = 2       # Discovered after last scan
```

**Natural ranking groups (used in `_natural_sort_key`):**
```python
_RANK_INITIAL_REVIEW = -3   # Unassessed subjective dimensions
_RANK_TRIAGE_STAGE = -2     # Epic triage workflow stages
_RANK_WORKFLOW_ACTION = -1   # Score checkpoints, create-plan
_RANK_CLUSTER = 0            # Auto-clustered findings
_RANK_FINDING = 1            # Individual findings + assessed subjective
```

The `_TIER_` vs `_RANK_` prefix makes it clear which sort layer each constant belongs to. Without this, someone looking at `_PLANNED = 0` and `_CLUSTER = 0` would wonder if they're the same tier.

One more thing: in `item_sort_key`, the `_TIER_PLANNED` branch returns `(0, blocked, stage_idx, ...)` for workflow stages and `(0, 0, plan_pos, ...)` for others. The `0` here is `_TIER_PLANNED`. Then for non-planned items, `group = 2 if is_new else 1` maps to `_TIER_NEW` / `_TIER_EXISTING`. The return is `(group, *_natural_sort_key(item))`. Since `_natural_sort_key` returns tuples starting with `-3` through `1`, the full key for existing items looks like `(1, -3, ...)` or `(1, 1, ...)`. This works because `_TIER_PLANNED (0)` < `_TIER_EXISTING (1)` < `_TIER_NEW (2)`, and within each tier the natural key provides sub-ordering.

With the constants, `item_sort_key` becomes:

```python
def item_sort_key(item):
    plan_pos = item.get("_plan_position")
    if plan_pos is not None:
        if item.get("kind") == "workflow_stage":
            blocked = 1 if item.get("is_blocked") else 0
            return (_TIER_PLANNED, blocked, int(item.get("stage_index", 0)), item.get("id", ""))
        return (_TIER_PLANNED, 0, plan_pos, item.get("id", ""))

    tier = _TIER_NEW if item.get("_is_new") else _TIER_EXISTING
    return (tier, *_natural_sort_key(item))
```

And `_natural_sort_key` becomes:

```python
def _natural_sort_key(item):
    kind = item.get("kind", "finding")
    if kind == "subjective_dimension" and item.get("initial_review"):
        return (_RANK_INITIAL_REVIEW, 0, subjective_score_value(item), item.get("id", ""))
    if kind == "workflow_stage":
        return (_RANK_TRIAGE_STAGE, 1 if item.get("is_blocked") else 0, ...)
    if kind == "workflow_action":
        return (_RANK_WORKFLOW_ACTION, 0, 0, item.get("id", ""))
    if kind == "cluster":
        return (_RANK_CLUSTER, ACTION_TYPE_PRIORITY.get(...), ...)
    # findings + assessed subjective
    ...
    return (_RANK_FINDING, -impact, ...)
```

This is a real readability improvement. The `0` padding positions in the tuples are still magic, but that's inherent to Python tuple sorting -- there's no way around it without a sort key class, which would be over-engineering.

### 3. Drop underscore prefixes in `plan_order.py` -- Assessment: Correct and straightforward

The import surface for the underscore-prefixed functions is limited to:
- `core.py` -- imports all 5 functions directly
- `plan_order.py` internal `apply_plan_order` wrapper -- calls all 5 internally
- `__all__` in `plan_order.py` -- lists all 5 with underscores

No external code (tests, app commands) imports these underscore-prefixed functions directly. The external imports from `plan_order.py` are only `collapse_clusters` and `new_item_ids` (both already public-named). So the rename is safe.

The changes needed:
1. `plan_order.py`: rename 5 functions, update `__all__`, update internal calls in `apply_plan_order`
2. `core.py`: update import statement (lines 34-38) and all call sites (lines 218-220, 229-231)
3. `ranking.py`: update docstring reference at line 208 (`_stamp_plan_sort_keys` -> `stamp_plan_sort_keys`)

That's it. Clean.

### 4. Group `QueueBuildOptions` fields with comments -- Assessment: Yes, do it

The dataclass has 12 fields. Grouping them makes sense:

```python
@dataclass(frozen=True)
class QueueBuildOptions:
    # Output control
    count: int | None = 1
    explain: bool = False

    # Scope filtering
    scan_path: str | None | object = _SCAN_PATH_FROM_STATE
    scope: str | None = None
    status: str = "open"
    chronic: bool = False

    # Subjective gating
    include_subjective: bool = True
    subjective_threshold: float = 100.0
    policy: SubjectiveVisibility | None = None

    # Plan integration
    plan: dict | None = None
    include_skipped: bool = False
    cluster: str | None = None

    # Pre-computed context (overrides plan/policy when set)
    context: QueueContext | None = None
```

### Your additional questions:

**"Should I do anything about `ranking.py` being 336 lines with `build_finding_items` in it?"**

I looked at the content breakdown:
- `enrich_with_impact` + `_compute_item_impact`: 60 lines -- these are scoring, not ranking
- `build_finding_items`: 58 lines -- this is gathering/filtering, not ranking
- `subjective_score_value`: 5 lines -- used by ranking, fine here
- `_natural_sort_key` + `item_sort_key`: 76 lines -- core ranking
- `item_explain`: 78 lines -- explain metadata, related to ranking
- `group_queue_items`: 14 lines -- grouping, tangentially related

The file is named "ranking" but actually contains three concerns: item building, impact scoring, and ranking. At 335 lines it's not painful, but the name is misleading.

My recommendation: **don't move anything now.** The file is at a comfortable size. But if you want to be precise, `build_finding_items` is the one function that doesn't belong -- it's item construction, not ranking. It could move to `core.py` (where it's consumed) or a hypothetical `items.py`. But this is a "nice to have" that adds import churn for marginal benefit. The file is already cleanly structured with the right `__all__`.

**"Is there anything else that would make this code beautiful?"**

Three things I noticed:

1. **The `all_items` variable name** is generic. Through the pipeline it accumulates, gets filtered, gets sorted -- it means different things at different stages. Your proposed extraction solves this by scoping the mutations inside named functions. After extraction, the orchestrator can just use `items` throughout (as in your proposal). Each mutation is a named function call, so the reader knows what changed.

2. **The `effective_plan` name** appears 12 times in the current `build_work_queue`. After extraction, it only appears at the top (resolution) and as an argument to each step. Your proposed code just uses `plan` which is cleaner. The "effective" prefix was needed when `resolved_options.plan` and `ctx.plan` were both in scope; once you resolve early, `plan` is unambiguous.

3. **The `apply_plan_order` function in `plan_order.py`** (lines 124-148) is now redundant with `build_work_queue`'s inline pipeline. After your refactoring, `build_work_queue` calls `_stamp_plan_keys` + `sort` + `_apply_plan_postsort`. Meanwhile `apply_plan_order` does `enrich + stamp + separate + sort + stamp_positions + filter_cluster`. These are almost-but-not-quite the same sequence. Check whether `apply_plan_order` is still called anywhere outside of `plan_order.py` itself.

Let me verify:

`apply_plan_order` is only defined and listed in `__all__` in `plan_order.py` -- it has zero external callers (the grep showed only the definition and `__all__` entry). So it's dead code that can be removed, or kept as a convenience for external callers who have pre-built items and want plan ordering without the full pipeline. I'd remove it and simplify. If someone needs it later, the individual functions are public.

1. **Named constants in `ranking.py`** -- zero-risk, no import changes, immediate readability gain.
2. **Drop underscores in `plan_order.py`** -- small, mechanical, update 3 files.
3. **Extract `_passes_impact_floor` predicate** -- small, self-contained, makes the list comprehension readable.
4. **Extract pipeline steps in `core.py`** -- the main refactor. Do `_gather_subjective_items` and `_gather_workflow_items` first (they're the cleanest extractions), then `_resolve_inputs`, then the plan pre/post sort wrappers, then `_empty_queue_fallback` and `_truncate_and_wrap`.
5. **Group `QueueBuildOptions` fields** -- cosmetic, do last.
6. **Optionally**: remove `apply_plan_order` from `plan_order.py` if confirmed dead.

Run the full test suite after each numbered step.
